| **Admin-ONLY routes** | **3 dispatch sites of 102** | `authorization_matrix.test.mjs`. `requireAdmin` has **4 call sites in the whole worker**, all in `admin.js`; ~190 other gates admit staff |
| **Live roles (production D1)** | **3 admin, 0 staff, 1 user** | live MCP read 2026-08-08. This is why S-2 is a loaded gun and not a fire |

**On the version number:** v0.99.0 → **v0.100.0**, not 1.0.0. A three-digit minor parsed cleanly
through preflight, the buster sweep (400/64, one value) and the changelog writer. **Whether the
next milestone should be 1.0.0 is the owner's call** — see §5; nothing blocks on it.

**v0.183.0 (iteration 124) — tournament-view link hardening (Gemini review, round 2; "review and
decide if correct").** Two valid edge findings applied: (1) the `aria-label` name prefix is now
CONDITIONAL in both `schedule.js` and `leagues.js` — a nameless event announces just the kind, not a
dangling " — "; (2) `leagues.js` checks `status === "completed"` EXPLICITLY (aligning with
schedule.js's `hasLiveView`), so a completed league with no parseable start date still links its
standings instead of falling to "Closed". `member_event_view.test.mjs` +1 net (name-fallback pins,
NC-5 for the dangling separator). REFUTED: the double-quote-escaping worry (both `esc()` map
`"`→`&quot;`). **RF-4's nine-entry collapse was MEASURED, not built:** the event-scoped rail set is
Tournament Ops / League Manager / Schedule Editor / Brackets / Divisions / Pool Board / Court Board /
Scoring Links, and **Schedule Editor is shared by both tournaments and leagues** — its single
PARENT-highlight mapping is a real decision. That, plus the ~40-page live-admin rail rewrite, makes
it a focused high-blast-radius unit; teed up as the next unit with the decision surfaced as a
question. No new page, route, or migration this session.

**v0.182.0 (iteration 123) — a11y on the tournament-view links (owner forwarded a Gemini review of
v0.181.0; "review and decide if correct").** Measured each finding. **APPLIED (valid, WCAG 2.4.4):**
the "Pools & bracket" / "Standings & scores" links now carry an event-name `aria-label` on both
`schedule.js` and `leagues.js` — a page listing several events otherwise gives a screen-reader links
list N identical link texts with no way to tell events apart. `member_event_view.test.mjs` pins the
name-bearing label + an NC that a constant label fails. **REFUTED (not a live bug):** "a cancelled
league would show a live link" — the public schedule feed's worker query is `status IN
('published','in_progress','completed')`, so cancelled/draft never reach the member list; a comment
in `leagues.js` documents why the date fallback is safe rather than guarding a state the server
prevents. **DECLINED:** an explicit `!isNaN(d)` guard — an invalid date already yields no link and
`starts_at` is a server DB datetime. No new page, no route, no migration. RF-4's nine-entry collapse
stays GREENLIT as the next unit.

**v0.181.0 (iteration 122) — members reach the tournament view from an event (owner req 2026-08-23).**
He asked whether members can see a read-only tournament display (pools, bracket, info) and enter
scores "if needed." MEASURED: the view already exists — `live.html?event=N` (`/api/live/events/:id`,
team names only, public) renders divisions→pools, brackets and standings, degrading gracefully for a
not-started event. The gap was the PATH — the RF-6/RF-9 class: `schedule.js` showed a started event
with NO action and `leagues.js` rendered a DEAD "In progress"/"Closed" label. Now a started
tournament/league card links to `live.html?event=N` ("Pools & bracket" for tournaments, "Standings
& scores" for leagues); gated to those types and to started events, so an upcoming event still shows
Register. No new page, no new route. Guards: `member_event_view.test.mjs` (7, real-source NCs).
**Also folded in from the forwarded Gemini review of v0.180.0:** the ◈ glyph on the Sub-Finder
buttons is wrapped in `<span aria-hidden="true">` (the rail icon was already hidden). Declined with
reasons: inline `text-decoration:none`→CSS (it is the house idiom on every `.btn` anchor) and a
`#subFinder` hash redirect (nothing ever linked that fragment). **NOT built — queued: members
ENTERING scores from the view** (no self-serve scoring-link path exists; staff-distributed today).
**RF-4's nine-entry collapse is GREENLIT** (owner 2026-08-23: "Yes collapse it and it's admin only")
— next unit.

**v0.180.0 (iteration 121) — Sub-Finder is its own module (owner req 2026-08-22; answers RF-10
half 1).** His word: "make it 1 button on the menu called Sub-Finder … additional links to that

Ops, loads admin-nav.js); which page he means is a genuine design question, queued below rather than

**Gemini review of 97c06a2/bd77660/12b5255 measured — no code warranted:** the auth regex keeps

**v0.179.0 (iteration 120) — RF-10 half 2: "your league tonight."** The member Leagues page
showed nothing about tonight while the public court+opponent payload had exactly one caller.
Now a signed-in member with a team in a live league sees a banner above the list — "Tonight —
<league>: you're on now on Court N vs <opponent>" (or up next, or plainly live) — linking the
live board. No new server surface: `/api/profile/teams` + `/api/live/events/:id`, matched by
team NAME because the payload deliberately ships no ids. One grouping judgement (`groupOf`)
feeds both the page's headings and the banner; the decoration rule makes tonight() un-awaited

half — the Subs rail entry — is deliberately NOT built: the member meets subs in three places

**v0.178.0 (iteration 119) — RF-5: the pool-remove affordance.** The measurement held: the
capability has existed since v0.70.0 and is the owner's own 2026-08-03 spec ("if it is empty,
itll auto delete") — save() omits empty zones, the server hard-deletes a never-played pool and
soft-deletes a matched one, clearing teams.pool_id for migration 0039's FK. What shipped is the
affordance and the wording, nothing more: a real ✕ button in every pool head (36px hit target,
keyboard-reachable, living in the head — which is not a drop zone, so a drag over the list cannot
land on it) that sends the pool's teams back to the WORKSPACE (rule 1: the staging area, never
nowhere), removes the zone locally and marks the board dirty — "nothing saves until you say so"
holds, and Save then omits the pool so the server does what the spec always said. The board says
the law under the keyboard tip ("Removing a pool (✕) sends its teams back to the workspace — an
empty pool disappears when you save"); the button's aria-label says the same. Guards:
pool_board.test.mjs v1.1, +4 watched red first — the affordance present and wired, THE ORPHAN PIN
(emptyPool must return teams to the workspace, remove the zone, mark dirty), the wording line,
and NC-P1 (stripping the workspace return fails the orphan pin). Suite 2135 → 2139. His item 5's
second sentence (pools → points → matchups → bracket) stays with RF-1's bracket work, as the row
records.

**v0.177.0 (iteration 118) — RF-8(b)/D-48: the expired bounce has a reader, and sign-in carries
you back.** Since v0.26.0 a dead staff session bounced to `index.html?expired=1&from=<page>` and
nothing read either param — a timed-out director landed on a bare card with no reason and no way
back; the writer was built, the reader never was. Now the login card says the session expired
(and that sign-in will land you back where you were); the sign-in request carries `from` and the
server embeds it in the magic link — the link may be opened on another device, so the LINK is
the only carry that survives; after verify, `location.replace(returnTo)`; the passkey path is
covered at boot because its reload keeps the query. The open-redirect line is one validator per
side: only a bare same-directory page name (`^[a-z0-9-]+\.html$`) is embedded in the emailed
link or handed to `location.replace()`; the eight hostile shapes tested (absolute URLs,
protocol-relative, traversal, path-bearing, javascript:, query/hash riders, encoded) are DROPPED
with a 200, never refused — sign-in must not fail over a malformed return hint. Guards:
`expired_return.test.mjs` (new, 7) — the carry tested BEHAVIOURALLY through the worker in
sandbox mode; 4 reds watched, 3 absence-shaped greens explained pre-fix. Suite 2128 → 2135,
test files 139 → 140. RF-8's remaining half: (a) the conditionally-bare brand lockup; (c) was
closed by v0.171.0's tablist removal.

**v0.176.0 (iteration 117) — RF-7: the member calendar cap, and a pager that fetches what it
shows.** "The calendar boxes are STILL not correct" — true for the member calendar, which never
got WF-1's cap while the admin one has been capped and guarded since v0.133.0; and its month
pager called render() over a fetch window pinned at today−7/+180, so paging past the window
showed empty boxes because of the REQUEST, not the schedule (the server honours any from/to —
re-measured, no server change). The cap is ONE judgement with two readers now, literally:
`BT_CAL` (DAY_CAP + split) lives in config.js — the one script both shells load — and BOTH
calendars render through it; the admin's local `CAL_DAY_CAP` literal is deleted deliberately.
Member "+N more" expands the day in place (`aria-expanded` maintained); a stale cached config.js
degrades to the uncapped pre-RF-7 cell for its ~10-minute window rather than a dead calendar.
The pager and the List/Month switch refetch, month windows derived from calCursor's 42 visible
cells. Guards: events_calendar.test.mjs v2.0 — BT_CAL is EXTRACTED from config.js's shipped
bytes and EXECUTED through the admin cell builder (judgement and builder tested as one
artifact), the one-judgement property itself is enforced, and the member reader is pinned
(split + graceful absence, "+N more", both pager buttons calling load(), the calCursor-derived
window) with NC-C1/NC-C2. 7 reds watched pre-fix. Suite 2123 → 2128.

**v0.175.0 (iteration 116) — RF-1(f): the bracket press says what happened.** His item 1's
sharpest finding closed, both halves. The engine answers 409 unless `replace:true` and Tournament
Ops never offered it — the second press was strictly silent in effect; now the press confirms with
the engine's own sentence ("…already has a bracket with N games…" / "Replace it? The current
bracket is set aside, not lost.") and re-POSTs with replace — the `admin-brackets.js generate()`
behaviour, which this page's own `plCommit` already spoke, routed through ONE `gen()` writer site
so bracket_rewire's one-writer uniqueness pin kept its licence unmodified. And the outcome no
longer renders into `#warningsBox` a whole grid above the button — it speaks at `#bracketNote`
beside the press (`aria-live="polite"`), while `#warningsBox` stays the schedule generator's
voice. Guards: bracket_rewire v1.1, +4 watched red first — a region verdict over the press
handler, the placement pinned in both directions, NC-F1 (strips the re-POST; its mutation targets
the press's own `gen()` call because a bare `replace: true` needle would hit `plCommit` first),
NC-F2 (points the outcome back above the fold). Suite 2119 → 2123.

**v0.174.0 (iteration 115) — D-53 found and closed: the `ends_at` writers exist now; picker ARIA.**
Measuring the owner-forwarded Gemini review of v0.173.0 surfaced something bigger than either of
its recommendations: NOTHING on the owner's path ever wrote `ends_at` — every non-NULL value in
live D1 was sandbox seed — so RF-4b's date rule, shipped the day before, would have read every
real event as active forever and his complaint would have quietly returned as data grew. Four
layers each looked correct and jointly guaranteed NULL: the modal never collected an end;
`patchEvent`'s allowed list would drop it silently (D-34's "Saved." class); `createEvent`'s
INSERT never named it; `EVENT_FIELDS` stripped it before `insertEvent`'s bind — which named it,
as dead code. Fixed end-to-end: the modal collects End date/time (optional; backwards end refused
in a sentence), the recurring form collects End-time-each-night and `createRecurring` derives
each instance's end from its OWN date (`endsForInstance` — a base datetime verbatim would stamp
night 1's end on every night), `editSeries` derives the same rule in SQL, and all three server
writers carry the field. Gemini's own recommendations, measured: timezone "High Risk" REFUTED
(the whole corpus is uniform naive-local, the writers keep that format, `nowStamp()` already
recomputed per render — the epoch rewrite was declined); ARIA CONFIRMED and shipped
(`aria-controls` + live `aria-expanded` on the Show-past toggle). Guards: +3 in
`admin_manage.test.mjs` v1.1, watched red first — the whole write path in one pin, the
derivation pin, the ARIA pair. Suite 2116 → 2119.

**v0.173.0 (iteration 114) — RF-4's management pickers + RF-4b's measured date rule; D-47 closed;
D-52 recorded.** His option C verbatim: *"tournaments and Leagues have their own buttons for
management that sorts which they can pick of a filtered list… the events that past must be removed
from the active management list. They should still be available in the events page…"* Two rail
entries — Tournament Management / League Management — land on one new hash-scoped page
(`admin-manage.html#tournaments` / `#leagues`, the `#roles` precedent); each lists that type's
ACTIVE events and every row opens the event's manager hub (`admin-manager.html?event=N`). RF-4b's
active/past rule was MEASURED before it was chosen: live D1 showed 4 of 7 events ended-by-date
with status still published/in_progress and the only `completed` row was sandbox seed — a status
filter would show every old event forever, his complaint itself. Shipped rule: past = ended by
date OR completed/cancelled; NULL `ends_at` stays manageable; "Show past (N)" is the override and
past events stay on Events & Programs. `GET /api/events` staff projection gained `ends_at` (the
column existed; the route never returned it). The nine event-scoped rail entries deliberately did
NOT collapse — next unit, after he verifies the pickers. D-47: the consent page (zero inbound
links since v0.25.0) got its home on the Waivers page. Guards: `admin_manage.test.mjs`, 7 tests
watched red first — rail parity across both sources, the rule EXECUTED from shipped source
against the measured trap with a date-clause NC, picker→hub seam, `ends_at` projection. Suite
2109 → 2116, files 138 → 139, sync-rail wrote 40 pages. **Also, no code: he chose Option 1
(as-is) on Gemini's v0.172.0 review; its claims measured anyway** — the sticky-header anchor
clipping is REAL and recorded as D-52 (he declined Option 2, so recorded not built); the
stale-HTML window is true but bounded (~10 min, degrades to pre-fix behaviour); badge "polling"
ALREADY EXISTS (`fillNavBadges`, v0.105.0).

**v0.172.0 (iteration 113) — the member menu in his order; RF-12 COMPLETE; D-19 and D-50 closed.**
His words: *"Realign that menu from most useful to least useful. Inbox should be 2 or 3, while
Home at #1, then notifications… Group things together that make sense."* The signed-in rail is
four groups now — You (Home · Notifications · Inbox, his order verbatim) · Play (Schedule ·
Leagues · Live scores · Community Play) · Explore (Explore · Player Library) · Account (My

12); the public grid's one signed-in route is named Explore (option B), which ends D-19's
two-homes collision. D-50: home.html's feed box — where notifications actually render — now
carries `id="notifications"`, and a new FRAGMENT CONTRACT in member_nav_paint v1.1 fails on any
rail anchor whose target id does not exist; the same sweep found and dropped a second dead
fragment (`index.html#signin`), with three inert page-script copies recorded as D-51. Five new
checks watched failing pre-fix, each red accounted for. Suite 2104 → 2109. **Also measured, no
code: the owner-forwarded Gemini review of v0.171.0** — Option 1 (no-cache HTML) impossible on
GitHub Pages (`max-age=600`, no header control; the network-first version-keyed SW already
ships), Option 3's "stale Manager tab → 401/403" REFUTED (`bt_login_role` has zero server
readers; a stale client self-heals within ~10 minutes), Option 2 (migration toast) wrong-scope
(live D1: the staff population is one person — the owner). Decision: no new code.

**v0.171.0 (iteration 112) — §-1r RF-12's security half: no admin affordance on any member
surface.** His words: *"There should be no admin access from this screen."* ENUMERATED FIRST — six
affordance sites across all 22 member-facing pages, the repo-root pages, and every local script
they load — then removed: the static `#btHdrAdmin → admin.html` anchor on 17 pages with
site-nav.js's role reveal; index.html's staff-gated Control Center card (this deliberately ends
D-22's owner-settled "exactly one way back" rule — his newer word governs, and the cost is stated:
staff reach the Control Center by URL or bookmark now); the login card's Member/Manager tablist
(measured: `bt_login_role` had no reader beyond the hint copy — one sign-in flow remains for
everyone, email link or the injected passkey button); and settings.html's System (staff) section,
whose `/api/admin/push/test` caller MOVED to admin-org-settings.html because deleting a route's
only caller would trip route_reachability's shrink-only ratchet. KEPT: the "Viewing as member —
Exit" pill — admin pages bounce back to home.html while `bt_demo_member` is set, so it is the
preview's ONLY exit; it is invisible to members and pinned by its own presence test with an
exactly-once allowance. The security position, said honestly: none of this granted access —
104/104 admin routes gated server-side — so these were affordances, not holes. The widest-set
guard derives everything (admin surface = any page loading admin-nav.js, which is what catches
tournament.html; member surface = every other shipped page + repo root; scripts = what those pages
load), strips comments at both grains with positive controls, and its script-src extractor was
probed before shipping — the first draft could not see past the `?v=` cache-buster and returned an
empty corpus, which the ≥20 floor caught. 13 pre-fix reds, each accounted for. Suite 2097 → 2104.
Live on both pipelines; served home.html/settings.html carry zero `btHdrAdmin`.

**v0.170.0 (iteration 110) — RF-6 and RF-9, the first two items off his refinement list, and both
were reported as broken screens.** Neither was a dead button. RF-6: the court board's `/move` returned
the board without the roster, and because the page is forbidden to patch its own board and reads the
withdrawn list from `data.roster`, one drag emptied that list and hid the "Back in" buttons — the only
undo for a mis-tap. Both 200 paths now carry the roster, the shape `/withdraw` already proved. **The
hole was in the seam between two guards that were each right**, so the new guard checks the CONTRACT:
the routes are derived from the client, the handler regions from the worker, both through the shared
extractor. RF-9: `index.html` was the 1 page of 56 whose theme toggle wrote half the state, and **the
widest-set guard that should have caught it could not match its own three exempted files** — it wanted
`getElementById("themeToggle").addEventListener` on one expression while every shell in this codebase
binds through a const. Theme is now judged as a property with no exemption list. Six guards, all
watched failing first. Live on both pipelines, 5 health samples at v0.170.0 with no flaps.

**NO-BUMP (iteration 108) — §-1c D-45 cluster 1: a commented-out org role filter was invisible to
the entire suite.** `org_honesty.test.mjs` exists to stop audit R1, the tester-round root cause where
the org switcher offered organizations the signed-in account holds no role in. Its four verdicts read
RAW source, so measured on the shipped `admin-nav.js`: with the role filter commented out,
**2083 of 2083 tests stayed green**, and the same with a module's 403 handler. The seven pairs the
sweep flagged turned out to be ONE guard reading seven files at ONE read site — count pairs to find
the work, then read the guard to find the unit. **THE RULE THE FILE NOW STATES: presence blanks,
absence stays raw.** `moduleVerdict` mixed both directions in one function — its `blame` field is the
retired "Couldn't load your events." sentence, an absence needle in a presence spelling — so blanking
the whole helper would have loosened exactly what the same file refuses to loosen ten lines later.
**Three adversarial reviewers caught that in the plan before it shipped**; `blame` stays raw and NC-12
pins the asymmetry with the measurement beside it. `default_org.test.mjs` pinned two of the same
literals and is fixed alongside. +8 controls, every one COMMENTING OUT a real line rather than
deleting it, plus the vacuity guards NC-1 and NC-2 never had. **A second defect came out of writing
those controls and is RECORDED, not fixed: §-1c D-46** — that file's forbidding `doesNotMatch` reaches
`all.some(o => …)` but not `all.some((o) => …)`, because `[^)]*` cannot cross a `)`. Widening a
forbidding assertion can redden correct code, so it gets its own unit. **No migration, no version
bump, no deploy.**

**NO-BUMP (iteration 107) — the suite-wide raw-source guard sweep (§6 item 0c), and the shared fix
it depends on was itself broken.** The unit was to ask how many guards besides the eleven mount
anchors are satisfied by code that has been commented out. Building the instrument first turned up
the larger finding: **`blankComments`, the shared answer to comment blindness that 34 test files
import, was deleting live code from 98 of the 118 shipped JS files** (D-44). Any `/*` inside a line
comment or a string opened a phantom block comment — `index.js:572`'s `// … /api/waiver/*` ate 155
live lines, 40 of the 43 dispatch-table entries among them. It is a lexer now, and the invariant that
catches this class is in the suite: blanking a comment cannot change whether a file parses, so
`comment_blanking.test.mjs` parses all 51 modules and all 67 browser scripts after blanking, and NC-9
runs the OLD body to prove the oracle can see the defect. **The sweep then measured the suite: 137
files scanned, 0 unclassifiable, 1,857 in-scope pairs, 122 reading raw source.** `resilience.test.mjs`
held 44 of them, including the one that matters — a commented-out dispatch entry was invisible to
every guard in the suite — and it is fixed behind a shared `dispatchTableIn`. The remaining **78** pairs
across 44 test files are D-45, with `worker/scripts/raw-source-sweep.mjs` shipped so the number is
re-measured rather than re-derived. **No migration, no version bump, no deploy — test and tooling
only.**

**v0.169.0 (iteration 106) — your own default organization (§6 item 1).** Migration 0052 adds one
nullable `users.default_org_id`, applied to live D1 before the push with the ledger read back.
**The foreign key is not the permission check:** `REFERENCES orgs(id)` proves only that the org
exists, so a role join proves it is yours and runs on BOTH sides — the write refuses an org you
hold no live role in, and the switcher consults the default only inside the role-filtered list it
already builds, so a revoked role degrades to the first-org fallback rather than granting anything.
Server-side rather than a storage key because `bt_org` already persists per BROWSER, and a
local-only default is absent exactly where it would be needed. **0 of 1 accounts hold one.**

**Two recorded citations in §6 were wrong and the quoted bytes were right in both** — which is why
neither had been challenged. `GET /api/me` was recorded at index.js:646-659 and is at 827; the two
literals that redden the suite ON A CORRECT FIX were recorded at admin-nav.js:624-625 and are at
785/788, with a third pin never recorded at all. Nothing was built on any of them unmeasured.


**v0.168.0 (iteration 105) — SG-3a: the module-bound staff gate.** Migration 0050 admitted the
`host` role and 0051 built the grant table, but nothing read it, so a host passed no gate and
reached nothing. `staffGateFor(...keys)` binds the module axis at the ONE mount site: the gate's
shape is unchanged, every one of the ~180 call sites is untouched, and no caller can forget the
axis because no caller passes it. It calls the REAL `requireStaff` first, passes a 401 through
unwidened, honours the F-1 acting-role drop, and widens a 403 only for a `host` holding a live
grant for THIS org and one of THIS mount's keys. A mistyped key throws at module scope.
**0 grant rows live — the deploy changed nobody's access.**

**THE SESSION OPENED ON 12 RED TESTS AND NOT ONE WAS A CODE DEFECT.** Both the S-1a gating ratchet
and the §-1e authorization matrix reported `tiers.js:244 /api/admin/org` as ungated — a route
gated the whole time — because `requireCoreStaff` was a fourth gating style neither scanner knew.
`gateCallsIn` and `gateKindCallsIn` each held a COPY of the pattern under a comment promising
they "can never drift"; prose is not a mechanism, and both now derive from one alternation. The
other ten reds were ten hand-rolled copies of one anchor, broken at once by the new call shape.

**v0.118.0 (iteration 42) — §-1i S-3c CLOSED: five security headers on every response the worker
returns.** CSP (`default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`,
`X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and HSTS, applied **SET-IF-ABSENT at the
fetch EGRESS** — the same choke point that merges CORS — **not inside `json()`, whose reach the
queue text overstated**: json() never sees the avatar bytes, the three CSV exports, the ICS feeds,
the SMS TwiML, or marketing's unsubscribe page (the only HTML the worker serves, the surface that
needs CSP most). `uploads.js` keeps its sandboxed CSP; marketing's page now owns a CSP that keeps
its inline styles alive. The dimension not touched is pinned in the same commit: an
out-of-allowlist origin still receives zero allow-origin headers. `security_headers.test.mjs`
(5 tests, each seen firing against the bare worker first; the calendar-404 test fails a
json()-level implementation by construction). Verified fully live, read-only. Residual named in
the roadmap S-3c entry: a page-side `<meta>` CSP for the GitHub-Pages HTML is a separate decision.

emailed"** — including failures where no link existed (the new 429, the pre-existing Brevo 502);

**v0.102.0 — §-1e priority 1: S-1a closed, and the ratchet that keeps it closed.**

`GET /api/admin/permissions` was the **first dispatch line in `adminRoutes`, above every gate**, with
no session check of any kind — it returned the role list and the whole capability matrix to any
anonymous caller. **No org data and no PII**, so severity is low, but it published the authorisation
model and made "every admin route is gated" false rather than nearly-true. Gated with
**`requireStaff`, not `requireAdmin`** — the least that closes the disclosure and the only choice
that cannot break a working caller; the one caller is `admin-users.js:286`, on a page whose own data
is separately admin-gated, so tightening further is a **reviewable decision, not something to slip
in under cover of a security fix**. Verified live: anonymous GET now returns **401**, with
`/api/health` at 200 as the control.

**`admin_route_gating.test.mjs` — 102 dispatch sites, all three legitimate gating styles modelled.**
It imports `routesFrom` from `route_reachability.test.mjs` as the route oracle rather than parsing
the corpus a second way, and asserts the two extractions agree shape for shape (C14).

**THE FIRST RUN, BEFORE THE FIX, DID TWO JOBS AT ONCE.** It named the real defect — so it is not
vacuous — **and named nothing else across the other 101 sites**, so it has no false positives, where
two earlier hand-rolled scans produced 25 between them. That is the whole argument for writing the
guard before the fix and watching it fail.

**AND IT CAUGHT ITS OWN BUG ON THAT SAME RUN.** It reported a *second* ungated route,
`consent.js:503 /api/admin/media-consent/*`. **Reading the handlers settled it** — both gate on
their first line (`consent.js:400,442`). The scan was wrong, not the code: that module splits the
dispatch across two statements (`mt = p.match(/…/);` then `if (mt) { … }`) and a region stopping at
the `;` never reaches the handlers. Fixed by following the assignment to the `if` that tests it,
**precisely rather than by widening the region — an over-wide region lets a neighbouring gated route
vouch for an ungated one, and being too permissive is the worse failure because it reports clean.**

Four NCs, each breaking real source: re-open S-1a from the fixed file · strip `pos.js`'s single
module-level gate and prove **all 11** of its routes are exposed, not one · prove a gate DEFINITION
is not a call site (the exact error behind the original false "clean") · prove comment-blanking
preserves offsets and line numbers.

**On the +12:** 7 tests are this file's own; the other 5 are `route_reachability`'s, which
re-register because importing its export imports a test file. Chosen over parsing the route corpus a
second, divergent way (C14) or refactoring a working guard mid-unit. **Stated in the file header
rather than left as a puzzle in the numbers** — if a third consumer appears, move `routesFrom` to
`worker/testkit/`.

**v0.101.0 — §-1d N-1, the member/admin shell ping-pong. TWO mechanisms, and the second is the one
that actually traps you.** From the owner's 2026-08-06 evening report: *"members page v admin page
seems to switch back and forth and expose the admin page."*

**(a) The member sidebar advertised the admin shell.** `site-nav.js` v2.15 pushed a `Manage` group
of four admin destinations into the MEMBER nav for any staff viewer, while the admin header links
back to the member site — each shell offered the other. Deleted; the single `#btHdrAdmin` header
link is the way back. Same change drops the `|| roles[0]` fallback that resolved a caller's role in
**another** org when they held none in the org on screen. Presentation-only both times —
`requireStaff` re-checks `userId + orgId` server-side — but presentation is what was reported.

**(b) THE BACK-TRAP — the real cause, and a grep found it.** `grep -rn "location.replace" web/`
returned **nothing**: every bounce in the codebase used `location.href`, which **pushes** a history
entry. A bounce off an admin page therefore left `[..., admin.html, home.html]` on the stack —
press Back, the browser re-enters `admin.html`, **repaints the entire static admin shell** (that
page ships its rail and every module name as markup, so it is on screen before `/api/me` can
answer), and `guard()` bounces forward again. **Back never escapes and the shell is re-exposed on
every attempt.** `guard()`'s four rejection paths now use `location.replace`. Deliberate user
navigations — "View as member", the two `history.back()` helpers — still push, because a user's own
click belongs in their history.

`header_actions.test.mjs` **+8 (1379 → 1387)**, each with a negative control that mutates the real
source: re-adding an admin href to the real NAV, adding `tournament.html` (which needs its own arm
because it does not match `/^admin[-.]/`), and restoring the `roles[0]` fallback. **No migration,
no new route, no new page.**

**Recorded, not built** (build-bias rule): roadmap **v1.5** adds **§-1d**, the navigation and
program-scoping program in the owner's verbatim words (N-1…N-6 plus three questions that must be
answered before anything is built), and **§-1e**, the security and penetration-test track, opening
with **finding S-1: two ungated `/api/admin/*` routes** rather than an empty heading. Three
further N-1 neighbours (N-1c rail links out to member pages · N-1d soft lockout when `/api/me` is
slow · N-1e the admin shell paints before it is authorised) are registered, not chased.

**Iteration 19 (no version bump — `worker/scripts` and `worker/test` only, neither ships to the
runtime): the Pages half of the release ritual got a check that can fail.** Three files:
`worker/scripts/preflight.mjs` v1.1 (a `pages` check + the pure `pagesVerdict`, reusing
`sweep-buster`'s own `versionFromIndex`/`bustersIn` rather than parsing the version a second way —
C14), `worker/test/preflight.test.mjs` (+5, suite 1374 → **1379**, file count unchanged at 85), and
`RALPH.md` **v3.1**. The negative controls mutate the real input: **NC-8** rewrites the real
`web/index.html` busters to `0.98.0` — the exact state Pages is in — and **NC-9 feeds the guard the
real repo-root `index.html`, the page the old ritual actually fetched, and asserts it can never
report `ok`.** The defect, as an assertion. See §0a for what it found.

**v0.100.0 — §-1b W-G, the fixture carries the owner's real registration shapes.**

### The recorded blocker was false, and one command disproved it

W-G was carried for **three sessions** as *"needs an interactive session — the Google Drive
connector is unavailable headless"*, and the previous handoff told this session to skip it on that
basis. **One `search_files` call proved Drive reachable.** The note had been true once and was
propagated forward on trust ever since. It also means **§5's "the remaining REVCO sheets need an
interactive session" was wrong** — the Spring sheet was read this session and the rest are readable
the same way.

Read: **2026 Spring REVCO 4's Tournament (Responses)** — 21 team rows, the same form as the
Valentines sheet already on file, plus a "2025 Dec Revco" folder left unread.

### The four shapes

The fixture is a product — the owner judges features against it, so when it is unrealistic they
cannot tell a broken feature from a broken fixture and reasonably assume the feature. Each shape
below is real, and each left a code path the seed could never reach:

- **A contact with NO email.** The "Emails of Teammates" cell is free text and rarely holds one
  address per player. With an address on every seeded row, `reachable` and `total` on the marketing
  overview **could never differ** — W-F's segments counted every contact reachable forever. Its id
  is **even on purpose**: every `contactOffset` is even, so captains are always odd ids and this
  person is only ever a team member. A registering captain always supplies an address.
- **`email-sent`** — the commonest real unpaid state (3 of 21 Spring, 4 of 29 Valentines). The
  registrations screen's Unpaid filter is `pending`/`email-sent`/`cash-pending` and the fixture
  produced only two of the three, so a third of that filter had never selected a seeded row.
- **`A/AA` alongside `BB/A`** — the only two labels the form offers. `tierOf` scores them 350 and
  250; **no seeded team occupied the 350 rung**, so no seeded comparison could span two tiers.
  "Block Party at A/AA" is lifted verbatim from the Spring sheet.
- **A team name containing a comma** — `Jarvis, Jork It A Lil`, real, and the shape that breaks a
  naive CSV split. Same class as the apostrophe in `Spike Lee's` that `q()` exists for.

Also corrected: a comment reading **"24 test people" over a 48-name list**.

`sandbox_real_shapes.test.mjs` **(+10, 1364 → 1374)**. **Every assertion proves the shape changes a
downstream answer**, not that a row exists — counting rows is what passed on the broken v1 seed.
A1 uses the exact `reachable` expression from `overview()`; A4 runs against the **real** `tierOf`
(exported for the purpose) rather than a copy of its regex table, and demands two distinct tiers.
Four negative controls each mutate the real seeded rows.

### The reusable finding

**A blocker is a claim, and claims decay. Re-test the blocker before you route around it.** The
"do not believe any document" rule was being applied to things being *built on* but not to the note
saying something *could not be built*. A recorded impossibility is the cheapest kind of claim to
re-check — one call — and it had been costing a queue item per session. **Apply the same check to
every remaining "blocked" line before treating it as blocked.**

### Previous session (v0.99.0), kept for context

**§-1b W-F — segments can target one event.** The engine, members screen and segments UI were all
already built and called; the gap was the join between them. `asEventId` coerces the string a
`<select>` posts, because a **dropped filter widens the segment to the whole org** rather than
emptying it. `marketing_segments_event.test.mjs` (+14). Deployed and verified live.

## §3. The tester round — state after v0.89.0

## §4. Owner answers of record — do NOT re-ask

**SETTLED 2026-08-27 — PLAYOFF FORMATS, THE RAIL COLLAPSE, AND THE PRODUCTION GATES.** His words:
*"For tournaments, it should be kock out bracket. For league we do placement pool (we call strenght
of power games) this may be a match or 1 set or game depending on time. 2. production gates: still
on hold, please investgate free versions of these and present. 3. Go with recommendation on menus."*
Three consequences, each measured before acted on (iteration 153):
- **A tournament ends in a KNOCKOUT BRACKET** — the existing tournament-side machinery
  (admin-brackets/buildTree); nothing new to build.
- **A league season ends in PLACEMENT POOLS ("strength of power games")** — rank-adjacent pods
  seeded by standings, each matchup a match / 1 set / a game DEPENDING ON TIME. Shipped v0.211.0
  as the pods fixture: `gamesPerMatch` 1–3 on the wins-pods path (3 = full match, best of 3).
  This SUPERSEDES the QC reference doc's larger pools→divisions playoff (its rec 3) as the league
  finale — the N-4a league-linked tournament remains available but is no longer what the league
  playoff needs.
- **A3 (rail collapse): "go with recommendation" = an explicit user collapse WINS over the
  active-group default** — measured as ALREADY the shipped behaviour (v0.206.0,
  admin_nav_collapse.test.mjs pins the stored-state-wins branch). Nothing built; do not re-ask.
- **The four production gates (Brevo · Square · Twilio A2P · VAPID) STAY ON HOLD.** He asked for
  free versions investigated and presented, not built — findings are in iteration 153's close
  (short form: Brevo has a 300-emails/day free tier; Square production keys are free, fees ride
  per transaction; Twilio A2P has no genuinely free compliant path; VAPID keys are self-generated
  at no cost). Opening any gate remains HIS move.

**SETTLED 2026-08-08 — THE TOURNAMENT-INSIDE-A-LEAGUE QUESTION. It unblocks §-1d, which had been
blocked on it since 2026-08-06.** The answer is **BOTH shapes, and the discriminator is where the
roster comes from**: (A) a **league-night tournament** played during a league night by the players
already there — roster is **the league's own teams**, no separate registration, and the owner says
it **lives inside the League module**; (B) a **linked open tournament** announced separately that
**anyone registers for**, with the bracket built from **whoever attends** — an ordinary tournament
that merely links back. **So "is Tournaments a tab inside League or a frame beside it" is answered
both ways, by the event's roster source** — a frame designed around either shape alone would have
been wrong for half the real cases. **Still to ask before building it:** what marks an event as
Shape A (a `parent_league_event_id`, or a flag plus a league reference) — **do not invent the
column**, and read §-1c **D-5** first, because `standings.rank` is an event-wide pool finish with no
division filter and `createBracket` seeds from `ORDER BY rank`, which collides with "draw the
bracket from the league's teams". **N-6 ("no bracket without pool") is a separate, still-open
question.**

**SETTLED 2026-08-08 (the three §-1f questions, answered in full):**

- **The admin/member switch is a REAL PRIVILEGE DROP, not a UI filter.** Owner: *"yes agreed drop
  privileges."* An admin "operating as a member" must be refused by the SERVER. **v0.103.0
  established what that costs and it is not a front-end change:** both gates query
  `user_org_roles` by `user_id + org_id` at request time (`index.js:331-343`, `admin.js:21-32`) and
  **nothing reads a role carried on the session** — so an acting-role needs a session column
  (migration), `buildCtx` reading it, and **both** gate functions honouring it. A drop that only
  `requireStaff` honours leaves the four `requireAdmin` routes open to a "member";
  `authorization_matrix.test.mjs` is what catches that.
- **No passwords. Ever.** Owner: *"ok"* to keeping the platform passwordless (magic link +
  WebAuthn). **The password-strength request is struck.** Do not propose adding passwords again.
- **Login branding is PER-ORG** — which **settles S-1b: `GET /api/orgs` stays unauthenticated, and
  that is now recorded as deliberate rather than unexplained.** The sign-in surface needs org
  branding before anyone has signed in. (Narrowing the public payload is still a fair improvement
  and belongs to the branding build, not to a security sweep.)
- **Cross-org product scoping.** Owner: *"the subfinder can be universal amongst all the orgs,
  player library, inbox, etc — leagues and tournaments can be nestled per org."* **This redefines
  §-1e priority 3**, which had assumed `org_id` on every read and write with no exceptions. The
  named universals are sub-finder, player library and inbox; leagues and tournaments are per-org;
  **"etc" is not a specification and the rest must be listed before that sweep runs.**

All of v1.18 §4 stands unchanged (direct commit authorised · CHANGELOG never hand-edited · waiver
system closed · SafeSport/M25 dropped · Twilio frozen · 12-on-5 · brackets auto-advance · pool floor
8 games, pools 6–11 · top division holds at 8 · seeding is a starting point · two views, no login on
the display · QR stopped · a hand-placed bracket slot is HELD · degrade, never collapse · grep the
single claim · KOTC §4.1–§4.4 · test range 90000–90999 disposable).
`docs/2026-08-04_reference_kotc-live-run_v1_0.md` stays trust-order-topmost for KOTC.

**Settled 2026-08-06 (do not re-open):** Offers are an **external system**, W-E.3 dropped, tryouts
ENDS at the squad board · **B5: keep `localStorage`, default org 1**, with an unbuilt follow-up to
let a user choose their own default org.

**Settled 2026-08-05:** Subs (excluded from tournament seeding; leagues count wins and divisions
played, never losses → **W-I**) · "Won before" = placement within division + tier-weighted score,
persisted ELO "potentially" → **W-H** · tournaments standalone once begun · **Supabase free tier**
under consideration, nothing built toward it.

**Settled 2026-08-08 (iteration 29) — THE END-OF-LEAGUE TOURNAMENT.** Owner: *"We do a tournament at
the end of the leagues. It changes based on participants and timeframe available."* This is Shape A,
and **it needs no new column and no migration.** The tournament is a **bracket on the league event**,
not a second event linked back to it. Three facts carry it, all asserted in
`worker/test/league_bracket.test.mjs` rather than read: (1) `brackets.event_id` already attaches a
bracket to any event; (2) `generateBracketFor` never reads `events.type` — a type gate injected as a
negative control turned 6 of the 8 tests red; (3) the Brackets screen's picker already lists every
event. **The roster question answers itself:** `teams.event_id` means the league event's teams ARE
the league's teams, so "the roster is the league's own teams" is structural, not a lookup.
**Do not build `parent_league_event_id`.** A separate event would need its own `teams` rows — the
league roster copied — and every no-show would then have to be fixed in two places.

**Settled 2026-08-08 (iteration 31) — THE UNIT IS GAMES, AND THESE ARE THE OWNER'S NUMBERS.**
*"however do not use time as the core unit of measure."* Time is a boundary (**7–8 hours for a
tournament, 6 is fine with fewer teams**), never the measure. The measure is **games per team**:

- **Target: ~8 games × 25 points in pool play**, *"before cutting anyone."* `MIN_GAMES_PER_TEAM = 8`
  in `formats.js` is that floor and has been since v0.62.0 — **there is exactly ONE definition of it
  and nothing may restate an 8.**
- **If pool play delivers fewer (6 or 7), EVERYONE BREAKS** so the first bracket games top them up.
- **Bracket games are 1 to 25 normally; sometimes 2 to 21**, *"depending on how we break them."*
- **A best-of-3 match counts 2.25 games** — *"25% chance of it going to 3 games."* **The quarter is
  load-bearing and must never be rounded away:** 6 + 2.25 clears the floor, 6 + 2 does not.
- ***"We try to break everyone possible to give them as many games as possible."*** A field short of
  the floor is therefore NEVER told to cut itself. **Top 8 (settled in iteration 30) belongs to the
  TIME boundary only**, and the time boundary is not the verdict.
- **N-6 ANSWERED:** *"Please ensure brackets are scored by pool play, that is the whole point of pool
  play."* The entry-seed fallback is not removed — a bracket-only event legitimately needs it — but
  it now announces itself, because a bracket seeded from entry order looks identical to one that
  came out of a real finish.


**Settled 2026-08-08 (iteration 30) — THE TWO KNOBS, ANSWERED AND SHIPPED IN v0.108.0.**
Owner, asked which lever gives when time is short: **"top 8"**. Asked per-division or league-wide:
*"depends on sign ups, the goal is to get everyone sufficient games (so we can double games in pool
play if needbe). Generally, 1 bracket across the league. but if we do large number of teams that are
unrated, then we will split based on divisions if there is a large skill variation."*

**THE MIDDLE CLAUSE IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS AND IT IS EASY TO READ PAST.** The constraint is a
**floor on games played, not a ceiling on minutes used.** A director who is short on time is not
asking "does it fit" — they are asking "what do I cut", and the answer is the bracket, because pool
play is where everyone gets their games. So the estimate suggests **top 8** on an overrun and never
suggests trimming pool play; and on an underrun it reports the spare time as **another round of pool
play**, which is the owner's actual goal rather than slack.

**League-wide is the DEFAULT, not the only mode.** The division split is the exception, reserved for
a large unrated field with wide skill variation — so `standings`' lack of a division filter (D-5) is
**not a defect against the default path**, and the default draw stamps no division at all (asserted).
D-5 becomes real work only when the owner hits the exception case; it is not urgent.

**What was already solved and must not be rebuilt:** "changes based on **participants**" is done.
`buildTree` takes any n, gives byes to the top seeds and never generates a play-in (owner, 2026-08-03:
*"we try to avoid pigtails"*); `a_size`/`include_rest` split A and BB so a tenth-place team still
plays; every slot is drag-or-type overridable for forfeits and people who went home (v0.91.0).

## §5. Owed by the owner

**ANSWERED 2026-08-10 (iteration 51) — five of the seven standing questions are CLOSED. Full text
and dispositions in roadmap §-1k; do not re-ask any of these.**
**(1) Passkey — DONE and VERIFIED IN LIVE D1** (1 credential, 1 user; was 0). **(2) S-4a — "not
necessary but ok"**, read as a yes with no urgency; the pinned "login succeeds WITHOUT the UV flag"
test must be REWRITTEN when it ships, and it must ship while sandbox login is still open, never as
part of closing it. **(3) USAV — deferred by the owner** to "towards the completion of the modules";
not owed. **(4) T2-1a undo — "hold until save, revert back and forward, confirm to save"**: the
deferred-persistence shape, and the pool board is the working precedent for it. **(5) Landing page —
answered BOTH ways: card grid for admin, `home.html` for members**, which settles D-19 by role
rather than by renaming; the rail order he wants is "1 Dashboard, 2 Inbox, 3 etc" and he asked for
**variations to choose from**, so it ships as a proposal like F-5/F-6 did.

**(6) CROSS-ORG — ANSWERED 2026-08-10, after four sessions, and the re-framing is what got the
answer.** Asked as an observable ("today a Boomtown member cannot see, message or find a sub from
another org — should that stay true?") the owner replied immediately: *"No, the subfinder should be
universal. messages should be universal with 1 account."* Leagues and tournaments stay per-org.
**Full analysis in roadmap §-1l, including the finding that reframes it: `users` has no `org_id`
and email is globally unique, so ONE ACCOUNT ALREADY EXISTS** — the fragmented thing is `contacts`,
one row per org per person, which makes **D-18 step one of this work rather than a tidy-up.**
**Two consequences still need his explicit yes before either feature is built, and they have not
gone away just because the direction is settled:** profile visibility was chosen inside one org and
going universal republishes those cards; and **blocks are org-scoped**, so going universal without
re-scoping them means the person you blocked reappears and can message you from another org. The
sub finder goes LAST of all because it *emails* matching members. **The lesson stands: a question
the owner cannot answer is a question we asked wrong** — this one took four sessions and one
rewrite of the sentence.

**ANSWERED 2026-08-10 — the team number is a THREE-TIER FALLBACK**, not one source: historical
performance of a returning team or player combination → the admin's own tile arrangement (1 top-down
per division) → registration order. **What shipped in v0.125.0 is tier THREE**, which was the right
one to build first because it is the only tier that always has an answer. **Tier 2 is the next unit
and it is small** (`board_order` + `division_id` already carry it), but it needs one decision stated
in the UI: tier-2 numbers legitimately change when the board is rearranged, so the number must be
**frozen at Save** rather than recomputed on every read, or the sheet in the director's hand
disagrees with the screen. **Tier 1 is blocked on a definition** — "returning team" does not exist
in this schema (teams are per-event, names are free text), so it means "a player combination seen
before", a fuzzy match with a threshold nobody has chosen, and it collides with **D-5** because
`standings.rank` is an event-wide finish with no division filter. Full table in roadmap §-1k K-1.

**STILL OPEN, iteration 51 — should sorting by level RANK the levels rather than group them?**
Today it groups by the registered label; ranking needs a level vocabulary (`BB/A`, `A/AA`, `Open`
are free text with no stored order), which is the same missing decision as the tag vocabulary below.

**NEW, iteration 52 — three questions from the partner marketplace scope (roadmap §-1m), needed
before M-1.** (a) **Is a partner an `orgs` row or a new table?** Recommendation `orgs` with a flag —
it already has the identity, branding and slug, and org 10 is literally named "External / Rental".
(b) **Do partner events appear in the main events list or only in the marketplace?** Mixed-in gives
discovery and a confusing "why can't I register here"; separate is clean but ignored. (c) **Is there
ever a fee, commission or paid placement?** If yes, that turns a page into a billing relationship
and is scoped separately.

- **NEW, iteration 31 — BEST-OF-3 IS AN ESTIMATE INPUT ONLY, AND THIS IS THE MOST LIKELY PLACE A HOLLOW FEATURE APPEARS NEXT.** The preview will tell a director that a best-of-3 first round reaches the 8-game floor, and running it is their action at the scorer table — but the generator writes ONE match per bracket node and does not schedule the extra games. Should it? If yes, `matches.game_number` already exists and the honest shape is N rows per node, not a flag.
- **NEW, iteration 31 — THE 7–8 HOUR CEILING IS A KNOWN NUMBER AND NOTHING USES IT.** Owner: *"We try to finish tournament within 7 to 8 hours (6 is ok with less teams)."* The preview only knows the minutes a director types into a box; nothing models a whole-day budget across pool AND bracket together. Worth building, or is the typed box enough?
- **DONE 2026-08-08 (iteration 32) — the five superseded docs are DELETED with the owner's explicit OK.** docs/ went 21 → 16 `.md` files. INDEX.md now carries one deletion record in place of four rows. Historical citations in `CHANGELOG.md`, `LOOP.md` and `docs/archive/` were LEFT INTACT deliberately — dated records, not navigation.
- **NEW, iteration 32 — POOL TIME IS NOT MODELLED, so the 7–8 hour day cannot be checked end to end.** `estimated_minutes` covers the BRACKET only. Worth building?
- **ANSWERED 2026-08-08 and SHIPPED in v0.108.0 — both timeframe questions are CLOSED.** Which knob gives: **top 8**. Per-division or league-wide: **league-wide by default**, splitting only for a large unrated field with wide skill variation. Recorded in full in §4; **do not re-ask**. **D-5 is therefore NOT urgent** — it is the exception path, not the default one.

  org.** Members set who can see their player card knowing the audience was that org; going

## §6. Next build — the loop queue (RALPH.md v3.1 PHASE 2)

0b. ~~**§-1q SG-3a — the module-bound staff gate**~~ — **DONE v0.168.0, live on both pipelines.**
   The role-CHECK rebuild recorded in §-1q was never needed and was NOT done. **0 grant rows exist**,
   so Cathy's account is now a data step rather than a build. `staff_gate_wiring.test.mjs` pins
   every mount in both directions, so a new module mounted without a binding decision is a red test.
0c. ~~**the suite-wide raw-source guard sweep**~~ — **MEASURED AND PART-FIXED, iteration 107. The
   fix this item prescribed was itself the defect.** `blankComments` was deleting live code from 98
   of the 118 shipped JS files (D-44, closed): any `/*` inside a line comment or a string opened a
   phantom block comment, and `index.js`'s own `// … /api/waiver/*` ate 155 live lines including 40
   of the 43 dispatch-table entries. It is a lexer now, with the parse oracle in the suite.
   **The sweep's numbers, instrument first: 137 test files scanned, 0 unclassifiable, 3,416 distinct
   text reads, 1,857 in scope, 222 comment-sensitive, 1,513 indifferent-or-vacuous, 122 raw.**
   `resilience.test.mjs` held 44 — including the finding of the session: **commenting out
   `["bracket", bracketRoutes],` in the real index.js left every guard in the suite green**, so a
   module could be imported, wired and never dispatched with 2,064 tests passing. Fixed behind a
   shared `dispatchTableIn`. **Iteration 108 closed cluster 1** — `org_honesty.test.mjs` (7 pairs)
   and its sibling `default_org.test.mjs` (1, the same two literals) — where a commented-out org role
   filter had been invisible to all 2,083 tests. **The remaining 70 pairs across 42 test files are
   §-1c D-45**: `signup_sheet` 5, `org_contact` 4, six files at 3, then a tail of 1–2. Re-measure with
   `node worker/scripts/raw-source-sweep.mjs --only=<file>`; `--self-test` proves the classifier on
   four fixtures before you believe a verdict. **Not every one of the 70 is a defect: an ABSENCE
   assertion is STRICTER on raw source**, and the sweep says so rather than guessing — the rule that
   came out of cluster 1 is **presence blanks, absence stays raw**, and one helper there mixed both
   directions in one function.

0. ~~**Land the deploy**~~ · ~~**§-1d N-1, the shell ping-pong**~~ — **BOTH DONE. v0.101.0 is live
   on BOTH pipelines** (§0b), each half sampled to convergence, `preflight` `deployed` PASS and
   `pages` PASS. Nothing is owed. **The tester round (§5) is unblocked and N-1 is the first thing
   to re-test.** Still run `node worker/scripts/preflight.mjs` at session start — it is one command
   and it is the check that was missing for two releases.
0a. ~~**§-1e S-1a + the gating ratchet**~~ — **DONE v0.102.0.** 102 of 102 admin routes gated,
   enforced every run. **§-1e priority 2 — the authorization matrix by role — is now the next unit
   in that track**: the ratchet proves a gate is REACHED, never that it refuses the right people.
   `requireStaff` accepts admin **or** staff and `requireAdmin` accepts admin only, and nothing
   checks that a route needing admin did not settle for staff. `admin.js` has both side by side and
   is where to start. **S-1b (`GET /api/orgs`, unauthenticated) is still open and still unasked** —
   it is probably deliberate (the sign-in surface needs branding); confirm who needs it before
   gating it.
1. ~~**Let a user choose their own default org**~~ — **DONE v0.169.0 (iteration 106), migration 0052.**
   **AND THIS ITEM'S OWN CITATIONS HAD ROTTED, which is the lesson worth keeping over the feature:**
   `GET /api/me` was recorded below at `index.js:646-659` and was at **827**; the two pinned
   literals were recorded at `admin-nav.js:624-625` and were at **785/788**, with a THIRD pin at
   `org_honesty.test.mjs:199` never recorded. Every quoted byte was correct, which is precisely why
   nobody had challenged the numbers. The prescribed safe shape held once the block was located.
   The measured detail below is kept as the worked example of a citation that rots — read it for
   the hazard, never for the line number.

   *(original entry, superseded)* **Let a user choose their own default org** (owner raised 2026-08-06). §6 called this "small,
   self-contained"; **iteration 19 verified the surface before designing and it is real work, not a
   missing screen.** Nothing exists — `grep -rn "default_org\|defaultOrg" worker/src web db/migrations`
   is empty. What was measured, so the next session need not re-derive it:
   - **`users` has no default column.** Live schema: `id, email, display_name, totp_secret,
     totp_enabled, created_at, updated_at, deleted_at`. **A migration 0043 is required** —
     `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN default_org_id INTEGER REFERENCES orgs(id);` Apply via
     Cloudflare MCP **before** the push, read the ledger row back. **localStorage cannot do this
     job**: `bt_org` already persists per browser, so a local-only "default" is only ever consulted
     on a fresh browser — where it is equally absent. It has to be server-side to mean anything.
   - **`GET /api/me` is `me(request, env)` at `index.js:646-659`, NOT in a module**, and is matched
     *above* the `startsWith("/api/")` branch, so it never sees `ctx`. It returns
     `{ user:{id,email,display_name,totp_enabled}, roles:[{org_id,role}], passkeys:n }`. Add the
     column to that `SELECT` and it reaches the client with no new route.
   - **A `PUT /api/me/default-org` DOES get `ctx`** — the top-level match is the exact string
     `"/api/me"`, so sub-paths fall through to the dispatch table (precedent:
     `PUT /api/me/sub-availability`, `announcements.js:245`). `ctx = {session, orgId, orgOk, userId,
     role}`, so **`ctx.userId` is the id to write** — do *not* copy sub-availability's
     `contactForSession`, which resolves a `contacts.id`, not a `users.id`. `json`, `audit` and
     `isStaff` are injected into every module.
   - **THE HAZARD, checked before choosing a shape:** the decision point is `admin-nav.js:624-625`,
     and **`org_honesty.test.mjs:154-157` pins two literal source substrings there** —
     `orgs.some((o) => Number(o.id) === current)` and `location.reload(); return;`. A change that
     rewrites line 624 into a helper call reddens the suite *on a correct fix*. **The natural shape
     is safe**: keep line 624 exactly as it is and change only the `current = Number(orgs[0].id)`
     fallback inside the block to prefer `me.default_org_id` when it is in the role-filtered set.
     `header_shell.test.mjs:85-88` separately pins `api("/api/orgs")` — do not replace that fetch.
   - **UI placement, and it is not the header.** The admin header is **38 byte-identical copies**
     enforced by `header_shell.test.mjs` (`variants.size === 1`); adding one element means editing
     all 38. There is **no user-account page** — `admin-org-settings.html` is the *org's* identity.
     Cheapest honest home is a row on `admin-org-settings.html` ("Make this my default org" for the
     signed-in user, clearly separated from the org's own fields), or a new page, which moves
     `sync-rail` + NAV parity. **`tokens.test.mjs:141-146` is a CEILING at exactly 20/20 with zero
     headroom** — any new page-level `:focus-visible` rule with an `outline:` not routed through
     `var(--focus-ring)` reddens the suite immediately.
   - **The in-memory D1 shim is `worker/testkit/d1-memory.mjs`** — *not* `worker/test/`, and its own
     header comment names a path that does not exist. `DB.query(sql, ...params)` is **synchronous**
     and returns a **bare array**; `DB.prepare(sql).all()` is async and returns `{success, results,
     meta}`. **There is no `DB.all()`** — that mistake has opened six sessions in a row.
2. **§-1c D-11** — add `e.org_id = ?1` to the `played` filter in `marketing.js`. Verified to need
   **zero test changes** (`?1` is numbered and consumes no bind). Cheap rider on any release
   touching that file.
3. **W-H** (persisted ELO — owner-gated, "potentially") and **W-I** (league sub record).
4. **§-1b W-G follow-on, now that Drive is known-reachable:** the "2025 Dec Revco" folder and the

   the four shapes the fixture was missing are now in it.
5. Then the v0.88.0 §6 remainder: net-empty redistribution · bracket disputes (owner-gated) · points
   cap + net labels (migrations, apply via MCP first) · roadmap §0 inventory · the D-4 remainder
   (LFG moderation, programs CRUD, POS sale detail, staff shifts, subs board, pass reverse/grants,
   facility check, divisions/assign, matches/*/court).

passing live probe would look like if the SQL were wrong: identical. So there was no live check to
run here, and pretending otherwise would have been the dishonest option.

copies it replaced (consent, member_portal, messages, profiles, registrations, calendar);
it joins `lower(u.email) = lower(c.email)` and consults `contacts.user_id` ONLY in ORDER BY.
**`messages.js:613 ownContact` is the holdout** — `(user_id=?2 OR email=?3)`, case-SENSITIVE
email, called at messages.js:158 and :546 — **and its own comment ("Same contact resolution
rule as member_portal.js") is STALE**, because member_portal's copy became the shared helper.
The two halves: **(a)** messages.js adopts the shared resolver (contactForSession rides
`wiredHelpers` — MEASURE whether messages' wire signature already receives it before adding
plumbing); **(b)** the AUTHORITY decision, which is the register's whole point: the shared
rule joins by email and only prefers a user-linked row — making `user_id` authoritative means
`(c.user_id = u.id OR lower(c.email) = lower(u.email))` with the linked row winning outright,
so an admin-edited address or a changed sign-in email cannot orphan a member from their own
record. **Live D1 fact (measure it fresh): 49 contacts / 1 user / no divergent case exists
today — the change is behavior-preserving NOW, which is exactly when to make it.** Guard
shape: behavioral, both paths — a contact with `user_id` set and a DIFFERENT email must
resolve to the linked row through a messages route AND a contactForSession consumer route;
a case-divergent email must resolve through both. Worker-only unit, but the release still
bumps and SWEEPS (the C6 rule). **After B20, §-0's actionable queue is EMPTY: everything left

**Standing rules:** sqlite_master before any NEW table · numbered SQL in `db/migrations/` only ·
migrations via Cloudflare MCP **before** the push, ledger row read back after · bump →
`sweep-buster.mjs --write` → `sync-rail.mjs --write` → preflight, in that order · docs-only gets no
bump and no sweep, but any release that bumps MUST sweep · every guard ships a negative control that
**mutates the real input** · assert call sites, never definitions, in both directions · byte-verify
the `index.js` bump as a one-line diff · **confirm BOTH halves of the deploy and sample to
convergence** · `git commit`/`git push` have gone first-try for two releases running — retry up to
three, but do not assume three are needed · **handoff §4 owner answers are settled — re-asking one
is a defect.**

**If a deploy misbehaves:** `/api/health` proves the **worker only**. Pages is a separate,
GitHub-managed pipeline with **no `workflow_dispatch`** — the only lever is a push to `main`. Two
releases running, one push created both runs and both went green in about a minute; that is the
healthy baseline. During the 2026-08-06 outage a push touching `worker/**` produced **no run at
all** (run *creation* was degraded), and `gh workflow run deploy-worker.yml` then worked first try.
**Do not debug the paths filter when a push goes quiet mid-incident — dispatch.**

**Owed by the owner** (§5, ask once, do not guess). **THREE §-1d QUESTIONS BLOCK THE ENTIRE
NAVIGATION PROGRAM** and must be asked before designing around them — v0.98.0's lesson is that an
owed question can DELETE work rather than unblock it: (1) a tournament inside a league — tagged to a
league and drawing its teams, or separate and merely linked? The owner wrote *"should potentially be
available"*, which is thinking aloud, not a decision. (2) N-6 *"we would not do bracket without
pool"* — hard refusal or overridable default? The standing §-1b rule says default. (3) N-2 — which
screen is *"the screen with the pop up"*? The audit could not single one out. **Also owed:** whether
the next milestone is 1.0.0 · a tag vocabulary, or a decision that there isn't one · a region
mapping for the area signal · whether to widen the shared admin type scale · **a fresh tester round
on Edge and Chrome — unblocked, and the shell ping-pong fixed in v0.101.0 is the first thing to
re-test (hard refresh required; the old bundle is cached under the previous buster).** B5 and "what
an offer is" were answered 2026-08-06 — do not re-ask. **The REVCO sheets do not need an interactive
session; Drive is reachable headless.**

**And above all:** do not believe any document about anything you are about to build on — including
documents that say a thing cannot be done, and **including a check that reports clean**. Ask what a
passing check would look like if the thing it guards were broken; if the answer is "identical", it
is not a check. **v0.102.0 is the worked example in both directions**: the guard it replaced
reported clean by reading a definition instead of a call site, and the guard that replaced it caught
a false positive in its own first output — which was only found by reading the code it accused. One
check, not a sweep; if the claim is about data, query live D1, then read the code that writes it.
